# Rigveda Samhita — Volume 29 (Kannada → English translation)

### Printed p. 1

Invocation verses:

वागीशाद्याः सुमनसः सर्वार्थानामुपक्रमे । (*vāgīśādyāḥ sumanasaḥ sarvārthānāmupakrame |*)
यं नत्वा कृतकृत्याः स्युस्तं नमामि गजाननं ॥ (*yaṃ natvā kṛtakṛtyāḥ syustaṃ namāmi gajānanaṃ ‖*)

यस्य निश्यसितं वेदा यो वेदेभ्योखिलं जगत् । (*yasya niśyasitaṃ vedā yo vedebhyokhilaṃ jagat |*)
निर्ममे तमहं वंदे विद्यातीर्थमहेश्वरं ॥ (*nirmame tamahaṃ vaṃde vidyātīrthamaheśvaraṃ ‖*)

वेदः शिवः शिवो वेदो वेदाध्याया सदाशिवः । (*vedaḥ śivaḥ śivo vedo vedādhyāyā sadāśivaḥ |*)
तस्मात्सर्वप्रयत्नेन वेदमेव सदा जपेत् ॥ (*tasmātsarvaprayatnena vedameva sadā japet ‖*)

प्राज्ञः श्रीसायणाचार्यो वाख्याय चरनेऽष्टके । (*prājñaḥ śrīsāyaṇācāryo vākhyāya carane 'ṣṭake |*)
स्पुटं द्वितीयमध्यायं तृतीयं वक्तुमुद्यतः ॥ (*spuṭaṃ dvitīyamadhyāyaṃ tṛtīyaṃ vaktumudyataḥ ‖*)

Ṛgveda-saṃhitā with the Bhāṣya of Sāyaṇa. Part 29.

In the eighth Aṣṭaka, the third Adhyāya.

The seventy-second sūkta in the tenth Maṇḍala.

## Sūkta 72 (Maṇḍala 10)
**NOTE**

**Introduction**

At the beginning of the bhāṣya on the first Ṛk of this sūkta, Sāyaṇa has indicated—आदितिदाक्षायण्यनेन सूक्तेन स्वयं यथादित्यानजनयत्तद्ब्रवीति (*āditidākṣāyaṇyanena sūktena svayaṃ yathādityānajanayattadbravīti*)—that is, that Aditi, the daughter of Dakṣa Prajāpati, herself tells in this sūkta the order of origin of her sons the Ādityas, or the gods. The sons of Aditi are called Ādityas or Āditeyas. It is the custom to call these very beings the gods and the dwellers in heaven. In the Kośa it is said आदितेया दिविषदो लेखा अदितिनंदनाः (*āditeyā diviṣado lekhā aditinaṃdanāḥ*).

### Printed p. 2

The order of origin of the gods that is told in the Purāṇas and the like differs from the order of origin of the gods that is told in the Ṛgveda. Although it is the custom to call the Ādityas by the general word "gods", the number of the Ādityas, that is, of the sons of Aditi, is limited. In some places this limit of number is stated in various ways as six, seven or eight. In the Taittirīya Brāhmaṇa and the Taittirīya Āraṇyaka their number is stated clearly as eight, and their names are also given. Although we explained this matter earlier, we shall discuss it at length in the later Ṛks of this sūkta, that is, in Ṛks 8 and 9. There are also some other gods who are not sons of Aditi. Their matter is not set out in this sūkta. Of the chief among them, Agni, Vāyu and the others, we have written briefly earlier about their origin; we shall explain it later as occasion arises.

In this sūkta the state that existed before creation, the nature of the manifest and the unmanifest, called sat and asat, and a brief account of the measures of time such as the yuga are indicated. We shall explain these matters in detail in the Special Topics of the respective Ṛks.

This sūkta was composed by the seer Bṛhaspati, traditionally famed as Brahmaṇaspati. In it the truth seen by Bṛhaspati and the order of creation of the world and the like are set out. Like the seers Aghamarṣaṇa, Dīrghatamas, Hiraṇyagarbha and others who set out spiritual truth, Bṛhaspati too is a renowned seer.

In this section, which is the sixth sūkta of the tenth Maṇḍala, Bṛhaspati has described the nature of the creation of the world according to his own direct vision. **NOTE** This description is not so helpful an instrument for exact philosophical knowledge. The language is profound and does not make clear what the seer's thought is. The bhāṣyakāra too has indicated his difficulty in this matter. Even so, it is one of the excellent sections that set out spiritual truth.

देवानां जाना विपन्यया प्र वोचाम (*devānāṃ jānā vipanyayā pra vocāma*)—"Let us praise the order of origin of the gods with our words of praise": this is the first resolve of the sūkta. In this very context an attempt is made to set out the primal principle of the world.

देवानां पूर्वे युगेऽसतः सदजायत । (*devānāṃ pūrve yuge 'sataḥ sadajāyata |*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-62-2) **NOTE**

The substance of this section is that in the first age of creation of the gods, sat was born from asat. The meaning is that the principle that was asat was the root of the world that is of the nature of sat. Here asat does not mean non-existence, a void in which nothing is. Sāyaṇa's explanation is that sat means a thing that has name and form and is embodied, and asat means a thing that is without name and form and is unembodied.

### Printed p. 3

The two principles sat and asat stand to each other in the relation of cause and effect; how can the principle of asat be the root of the phenomenal world? What is its true nature? This will be told later in the Special Topics of the sūkta नासदासीत् (*nāsadāsīt*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-129). The exact meaning of the word yuga, which indicates the time of the creation of the world, has to be examined in the present context. What is meant by the earlier yuga and the later yuga? What is the primal consciousness behind these divisions of yuga and behind the creation and the like that took place in each yuga? What is its resolve? These and other matters also have to be examined.

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> तत्र देवानामिति नवर्चमनुवाकापेक्षया चतुर्थं सूक्तमानुष्टुभं
> देवदेवत्यं । लोकनाम्नः पुत्रो बृहस्पतिरांगिरस एव वा बृहस्पतिर्यषिः । अथवा
> दक्षस्य दुहितादितियृषिः। तथा चानुक्रांतं। देवानां नव लौक्यो वा बृहस्पतिर्दाक्षायण्यदितिर्वा दैवमानुष्टुभमिति । गतः सूक्तविनियोगः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa says that, counting by anuvākas, this is the fourth sūkta of the nine-Ṛk anuvāka beginning देवानां (*devānāṃ*). It is in the Anuṣṭubh metre and its deity is the company of gods. The seer is Bṛhaspati, son of one named Loka, or else Bṛhaspati Āṅgirasa; or else the seer is Aditi, the daughter of Dakṣa. He supports this by citing the Anukramaṇī, which gives the same alternatives of seer, together with the deity of the gods and the Anuṣṭubh metre. The application of the sūkta in ritual has been stated already.

Anuvāda: As the seventy-one sūktas of the tenth Maṇḍala were completed in the previous part, the seventy-second sūkta, beginning देवानां (*devānāṃ*), is now begun. It is the fourth sūkta in the sixth Anuvāka of the tenth Maṇḍala. It has nine Ṛks. The seer of this sūkta is Bṛhaspati, son of the seer Loka, or Bṛhaspati, son of the seer Aṅgiras; or else the goddess Aditi, daughter of Dakṣa Prajāpati, is the seer; the deity is the gods; the metre is Anuṣṭubh. In the Anukramaṇī it is said देवानां नव लौक्यो वा बृहस्पतिर्दाक्षायण्यदितिर्वा दैवमानुष्टुभं (*devānāṃ nava laukyo vā bṛhaspatirdākṣāyaṇyaditirvā daivamānuṣṭubhaṃ*). The application in ritual has been stated earlier.

Maṇḍala 10; Anuvāka 6; Sūkta 72.
Aṣṭaka 8; Adhyāya 3; Varga 1, 2.
Number of Ṛks in the sūkta: 9.
Seer: बृहस्पतिर्बृहस्पतिर्वा लौक्य आदितिर्वा दाक्षायणी (*bṛhaspatirbṛhaspatirvā laukya āditirvā dākṣāyaṇī*) **NOTE**
Deity: देवा (*devā*).
Metre: अनुष्टुप् (*anuṣṭup*).

#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> दे॒वानां॒ नु व॒यं जाना॒ प्र वो॑चाम विप॒न्यया॑ ।  
> उ॒क्षेषु॑ श॒स्यमा॑नेषु॒ यः पश्या॒दुत्त॑रे यु॒गेः ॥ १ ॥

### Printed p. 4

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> दैवानां । नु । वयं । जाना । प्र । वोचाम । विपन्यया ।  
> उक्तेषु । शस्यमानेषु । यः । पश्यात् । उत्तरे । युगे ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अदितिर्दाक्षायण्यनेन सूक्तेन स्वयं यथादित्यानजनयत्तद्ववीति । बृहस्पत्यृषिपक्षे स ऋषिरदितेः सकाशादादित्योत्पत्ति प्रकारमाह । वयं देवानामादित्यानां जाना जन्मानि प्र वोचाम । प्रकथयाम । विपन्यया विस्पष्टया वाचा ।
> वयमिति वोचामेति चोभयत्र पूजार्थं बहुवचनं । आधैकवदाह । यो
> देवानां गणः पूर्व युग उत्पन्नोऽपुक्षेषु शस्यमानेषु यागे शस्त्रेष्टनुष्ठीयमानेषूत्तरे युगे वर्तमानं स्तुवंतं स्तोतारं पश्यात् पश्यति । अनेकेष्टपि
> युगेषु गतेषु कर्मसु स्तूयमानो वर्तत इत्यर्थः ।

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Aditi, daughter of Dakṣa, tells by this sūkta how she herself bore the Ādityas. On the view that the seer is Bṛhaspati, that seer tells from Aditi's side the manner of the origin of the Ādityas. "We", he says, proclaim the births of the gods, the Ādityas, with a clear speech: विपन्यया (*vipanyayā*) means clear, distinct speech. The plural in वयं (*vayaṃ*) and in वोचाम (*vocāma*) is used in both places out of reverence. Then he speaks of the single company: that company of gods which was born in the earlier age sees the worshipper who is present in the later age and is praising, when the praises are being recited in the sacrifice and the śastras are being performed. The meaning is that, many ages having passed, it remains praised in the rites.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- यः (*yaḥ*)—(though born in the earlier ages) which company of gods
- उत्तरे युगे (*uttare yuge*)—in the later ages
- उक्तेषु (*ukteṣu*)—the praises that are of the form of utterance
- शस्यमानेषु (*śasyamāneṣu*)—at the times when they are recited
- पश्यत् (*paśyat*)—which can look upon us, such (a company)
- देवानां (*devānāṃ*)—of the gods
- जाना (*jānā*)—the orders of origin
- वयं (*vayaṃ*)—we
- नु (*nu*)—now
- विपन्यया (*vipanyayā*)—with clear words of praise
- प्र वोचाम (*pra vocāma*)—let us praise and recite

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Those gods, the Ādityas and others, who, although born in the earlier ages, can still look upon men with a gracious eye now and in the later ages, when the praise-mantras that praise them are recited, the orders of origin of such gods, the Ādityas and others, let us now praise and recite with clear words of praise.

##### English translation
Let us proclaim with a clear voice the generations of the gods (the divine company), who, when their praises are recited, look (favourably on the worshipper) in this latter age.

### Printed p. 5

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

It is said that the seer of this Ṛk is Bṛhaspati, son of the seer Loka, or Bṛhaspati, son of the seer Aṅgiras, or Aditi, the daughter of Dakṣa. The idea in saying that any one of these may be the seer of this sūkta is that any one of the three may have set out the matter that is explained in this sūkta. If it is taken that Aditi is the seer of this sūkta, it is quite natural that she herself should tell the order of birth of her sons the Ādityas. When it is said that Bṛhaspati is the seer, it may be understood that the seer Bṛhaspati announces that he will tell the order of origin of the gods and, accordingly, sets out the order of their birth.

देवानां नु जाना (*devānāṃ nu jānā*)—आदित्यानां जन्मानि (*ādityānāṃ janmāni*). How were the Ādityas born in Aditi? How many are they? When were they born? To set out such matters the seer has used here the word जाना (*jānā*), that is जन्मानि (*janmāni*).

विपन्यया (*vipanyayā*)—विस्पष्टया वाचा (*vispaṣṭayā vācā*). With clear sentences; the seer declares that he will set out the matter with sentences whose explanation is so clear that there is no room for doubt.

वयं (*vayaṃ*)—"We": the seer is one, yet he uses the plural वयं (*vayaṃ*); is that not incorrect? In answer to this the bhāṣyakāra says वयमिति वोचामेति चोभयत्र पूजार्थं बहुवचनं (*vayamiti vocāmeti cobhayatra pūjārthaṃ bahuvacanaṃ*): here the two words वयं (*vayaṃ*) and वोचाम (*vocāma*) are used in the plural for the sake of honour. Besides, it is still the custom that poets and authors use the plural in their works where the singular of an individual would be used. Therefore the use of the plural here is correct; this is the meaning.

उक्तेषु शस्यमानेषु यः उत्तरे युगे पश्यात् (*ukteṣu śasyamāneṣu yaḥ uttare yuge paśyāt*) (तस्यजाना प्र वोचाम (*tasyajānā pra vocāma*))—यः देवानां गणः यागकाले शस्त्रेष्टनुष्ठीयमानेषु वर्तमानं स्तुवंतं स्तोतारं पश्यति (*yaḥ devānāṃ gaṇaḥ yāgakāle śastreṣṭanuṣṭhīyamāneṣu vartamānaṃ stuvaṃtaṃ stotāraṃ paśyati*) (तं देवगणस्य जन्मनि प्रकथयाम (*taṃ devagaṇasya janmani prakathayāma*)). When a sacrifice is performed, it is the custom, at the time of invoking the gods, to praise the gods' excellences and so invoke them. It is proper to set out the order of the gods' birth at such a time. Therefore the seer describes here that, while the praise-mantras are being recited and the gods are being praised, he will tell the order of origin of the gods. If one asks whether describing the order of birth of those gods amounts to singing their excellences, this is described in the second half of this Ṛk. In the later Ṛks, that is, in Ṛks 2 and 3, it is said that the birth of the gods took place at the time of creation, before the creation of all others; that is, the creation of

### Printed p. 6

men and other living beings took place long after the gods were created. Yet, that is, although created long ago, the gods too, it is said here, can even now look upon the sacrifices and other rites that men perform. That is, the gods are not short-lived like men and others; the span of their life is very long; they can live for a long time. It is with the aim of describing such excellences of the gods that the seer, in the second half of this Ṛk, describes the limit of the gods' life by comparison with the limit of the life of men.

उत्तरे युगे (*uttare yuge*)—अनेकेष्टपि युगेषु गतेषु कर्मसु स्तूयमानो वर्तत इत्यर्थः (*anekeṣṭapi yugeṣu gateṣu karmasu stūyamāno vartata ityarthaḥ*). In the following Ṛks 2 and 3 the words पूर्व युगे (*pūrva yuge*) and प्रथमे युगे (*prathame yuge*) are used. That is, the beginning of creation is called the first yuga or the earlier yuga; the meaning that appears here is that the yugas that go on after it and pass away, and the yuga that is now going on, are called the later yuga. Although the word yuga is used in some places in the Ṛgveda, they are used in different meanings. Only in the present Ṛk is the word yuga used in the meaning of a great division of time. It is therefore fitting to examine here the various meanings of the word yuga.

**The various meanings and uses of the word yuga**

The word yuga, in the meaning of a measure of time, is described at length in the Purāṇas and the like. Among them, the order in the Bhāgavata (Skṛ. 3-11-1) **NOTE** and in the Viṣṇu Purāṇa is as follows.

Vidura said:

यदात्थ ब्रह्मरूपस्य हरेरद्भुतकर्मणः । कालाख्यं लक्षणं ब्रह्मन्यथा वर्णय नः प्रभो ॥ (*yadāttha brahmarūpasya hareradbhutakarmaṇaḥ | kālākhyaṃ lakṣaṇaṃ brahmanyathā varṇaya naḥ prabho ‖*)
(Śrī. Bhā. 3-10-10) **NOTE**

"O great seer, you have described the great deeds of Hari, who is of the form of Brahman. In the present context, how has Hari, the original cause of the creation of the world, divided the principle that is of the form of time, in order to fix the measure of creation, continuance and the like? What is the nature of time? Explain it and tell us," so Vidura asks in prayer. In answer to it, Maitreya sets out the principle that is of the form of time in this way—

### Printed p. 7

Maitreya said:

गुणव्यतिकराकारो निर्विशेषोऽप्रतिष्ठितः । पुरुषस्तदुपादानमात्मानं लीलयासृजत् ॥ (*guṇavyatikarākāro nirviśeṣo 'pratiṣṭhitaḥ | puruṣastadupādānamātmānaṃ līlayāsṛjat ‖*)
(Śrī. Bhā. 3-10-11)

The great Prakṛti, which has an eternal state, undergoing modifications and changing, is what is called time. This time has no form, and no birth or death. But only in ordinary usage does this time have divisions. Making this time the instrumental cause, the Lord created the whole world as His sport.

विश्वं वै ब्रह्मतन्मात्रं संस्थितं विष्णु मायया । ईश्वरेण परिच्छिन्नं कालेनाव्यक्तमूरिना ॥ यथेदानीं तथाग्रे च पश्चादप्येतदीदृशं । सर्गो नवविधस्तस्य प्राकृतो वैकृतस्तु यः ॥ (*viśvaṃ vai brahmatanmātraṃ saṃsthitaṃ viṣṇu māyayā | īśvareṇa paricchinnaṃ kālenāvyaktamūrinā ‖ yathedānīṃ tathāgre ca paścādapyetadīdṛśaṃ | sargo navavidhastasya prākṛto vaikṛtastu yaḥ ‖*)
(Śrī. Bhā. 3.10-12, 13)

The whole world that undergoes creation is full of Brahman. For this world created by the māyā of Brahman, time, which is of unmanifest nature and is the controller of the whole world, is the cause of all. The division of time is only for the transactions of this world. But eternal time has no divisions. By the power of this time that controls the world there is the ninefold creation and also the tenth creation, called prākṛta-vaikṛta.

कालद्रव्यगुणैरस्य त्रिविधः प्रतिसंक्रमः । आद्यस्तु महतस्सर्गो गुणवैषम्यमात्मनः ॥ द्वितीयस्त्वहनो यत्र द्रव्यज्ञानक्रियोदयः । भूतसर्गस्तृतीयस्तु तन्मात्रो द्रव्यशक्तिमान् ॥ चतुर्थ ऐन्द्रियः सर्गो यस्तु ज्ञानक्रियात्मकः । वैकारिको देवसर्गः पंचमो यन्मयं मनः ॥ षष्ठस्तु तमसः सर्गो यस्त्वबुद्धिकृतः प्रभो । षडिमे प्राकृताः सर्गा वैकृतानपि मे शृणु ॥ (*kāladravyaguṇairasya trividhaḥ pratisaṃkramaḥ | ādyastu mahatassargo guṇavaiṣamyamātmanaḥ ‖ dvitīyastvahano yatra dravyajñānakriyodayaḥ | bhūtasargastṛtīyastu tanmātro dravyaśaktimān ‖ caturtha aindriyaḥ sargo yastu jñānakriyātmakaḥ | vaikāriko devasargaḥ paṃcamo yanmayaṃ manaḥ ‖ ṣaṣṭhastu tamasaḥ sargo yastvabuddhikṛtaḥ prabho | ṣaḍime prākṛtāḥ sargā vaikṛtānapi me śṛṇu ‖*)
(Śrī. Bhā. 3-10-14-17)

According to the rule of eternal time there are three kinds of dissolution: the daily dissolution, of the nature of deep sleep, that occurs in the time fixed for creation; the occasional dissolution caused by causes such as the fire from the mouth of Saṅkarṣaṇa; and the dissolution of Prakṛti, arising from the state of equilibrium of the guṇas, sattva and the others. Thus there are three

### Printed p. 8

kinds. By the resolve of the Supreme Self, among the creations, the Mahat-principle, which is the disturbance of the equilibrium of the guṇas, is the first creation. The ahaṅkāra, the cause of the creation of the elements, the senses and the deities, is the second creation. The subtle elements, which are the cause of the subtle elements (tanmātras) such as sound and of the gross elements such as earth, are the third creation. The creation of the senses, of the form of knowledge and action, is the fourth. The creation of the deities that preside over the senses and the like, and of the mind, is the fifth. The creation of ignorance is the sixth. These are the six creations born of Prakṛti. Now I shall tell of the creations born of Vikṛti.

रजोभाजो भगवतो लीलेयं हरिमेधसः । सप्तमो मुख्यसर्गस्तु षड्विधस्तस्थुषां च यः ॥ (*rajobhājo bhagavato līleyaṃ harimedhasaḥ | saptamo mukhyasargastu ṣaḍvidhastasthuṣāṃ ca yaḥ ‖*)
(Śrī. Bhā. 3-10-88) **NOTE**

The creation of the immovables is the seventh. It is the controller of time. It is mere sport of Brahman. It is of six kinds: the herbs and the like that are without consciousness. The eighth is the animals. In this creation there are twenty-one kinds of creatures. They have no knowledge of time. The human race is the ninth. The three creations, of man, of animals and of immovables, are the vaikṛta ones. Only the creation of Sanatkumāra and the others is of both natures, prākṛta and vaikṛta.
(Śrī. Bhā. 3-10-19-24)

वैकृतास्त्रय एवैते देवसर्गश्च सत्तम । वैकारिकस्तु यः प्रोक्तः कौमारस्तूभयात्मकः ॥ देवसर्गश्चाष्टविधो विबुधाः पितरोऽसुराः । गंधर्वाप्सरसः सिद्धा यक्षरक्षांसि चारणाः ॥ (*vaikṛtāstraya evaite devasargaśca sattama | vaikārikastu yaḥ proktaḥ kaumārastūbhayātmakaḥ ‖ devasargaścāṣṭavidho vibudhāḥ pitaro 'surāḥ | gaṃdharvāpsarasaḥ siddhā yakṣarakṣāṃsi cāraṇāḥ ‖*)
(Śrī. Bhā. 3-10-25-27)

The eight kinds of groups, the gods, the asuras, the gandharvas and the rest, belong to the vaikṛta creation. In this way the cosmic egg, which is a transformation of the guṇas, by the greatness of the Lord is born all at once, without before and after, as bubbles arise at one time from the water flowing in a river.

आतः परं प्रवक्ष्यामि वंशान् मन्वंतराणि च । एवं रजुप्लुत सृष्टा कल्पादिष्टात्मभूर्हरिः ॥ सृजत्यमोघसंकल्प आत्मैवात्मानमात्मना ॥ (*ātaḥ paraṃ pravakṣyāmi vaṃśān manvaṃtarāṇi ca | evaṃ rajupluta sṛṣṭā kalpādiṣṭātmabhūrhariḥ ‖ sṛjatyamoghasaṃkalpa ātmaivātmānamātmanā ‖*)
(Śrī. Bhā. 3-10.28-29)

Thus the order of creation is of two kinds. Now I shall tell the genealogies of the gods and the Manvantaras. The gods and asuras who in the first kalpa are famed by names such as Indra, those same ones in the different Manvantaras too are endowed with the same names and forms. Thus, through the marks of time and through the change of the guṇas, the tenfold creation came about. Now that same

### Printed p. 9

special nature of time has to be told. Therefore, saying "I shall tell the nature of the atom (paramāṇu), which measures and shows that time", Maitreya has told the nature of the division of time in this way. This division shows the division of time, place and objects.

Maitreya said:

चरमः सद्विशेषाणामनेकोऽसंयुतः सदा । परमाणुः स विज्ञेयो नृणामैक्यभ्रमो यतः ॥ सत एव पदार्थस्य स्वरूपावस्थितस्य । यत् । कैवल्यं परममहानविशेषो निरंतरः ॥ एवं कालोऽप्यनुमितः सौक्ष्मी स्थौल्ये च सत्तम । संस्थानभुक्ता भगवानव्यक्तो व्यक्तभुग्विभुः ॥ स कालः परमाणुर्वै यो भुक्ते परमाणुतां । ततोऽविशेषभुग्यस्तु स कालः परमो महान् ॥ (*caramaḥ sadviśeṣāṇāmaneko 'saṃyutaḥ sadā | paramāṇuḥ sa vijñeyo nṛṇāmaikyabhramo yataḥ ‖ sata eva padārthasya svarūpāvasthitasya | yat | kaivalyaṃ paramamahānaviśeṣo niraṃtaraḥ ‖ evaṃ kālo 'pyanumitaḥ saukṣmī sthaulye ca sattama | saṃsthānabhuktā bhagavānavyakto vyaktabhugvibhuḥ ‖ sa kālaḥ paramāṇurvai yo bhukte paramāṇutāṃ | tato 'viśeṣabhugyastu sa kālaḥ paramo mahān ‖*)
(Śrī. Bhā. 3.11.1-4)

If one goes on dividing the objects in the world, that part which cannot be divided any further, which is the extremely small part that neither changes nor forms groups, and whose combined sum people call objects, is called the atom (paramāṇu). Those very atoms, becoming a sum through change, come to be called the supremely great (parama-mahat) in the form of the common world free of distinctions. Just as the thing that indicates the measure of time receives names in this way, in the subtle state as the atom, in the middle state as objects, and in the gross state, in the form of the cosmic egg, as the supremely great, so also time, which is the cause of the change of objects and, though itself imperceptible, pervades perceptible things and is of the form of the power of the Lord, receives different names through the gross, subtle and middle states.

अणुदौव परमाणू स्यात्प्रसरेणुस्त्रयः स्मृतः । जलार्करश्मवगतः स्वमेवानुपतन्नगात् ॥ (*aṇudauva paramāṇū syātprasareṇustrayaḥ smṛtaḥ | jalārkaraśmavagataḥ svamevānupatannagāt ‖*)
(Śrī. Bhā. 3-11-4)

In this world the time that has attained the state of the atom is known as the atom, and the time that pervades this whole world is known as the supremely great. The order of its divisions is as follows—

त्रसरेणुत्रिकं भुंक्ते यः कालः स त्रुटिः स्मृतः । शतभागस्तु वेधः स्यात्तैस्त्रिभिस्तु लवः स्मृतः ॥ (*trasareṇutrikaṃ bhuṃkte yaḥ kālaḥ sa truṭiḥ smṛtaḥ | śatabhāgastu vedhaḥ syāttaistribhistu lavaḥ smṛtaḥ ‖*) **GLOSS**

### Printed p. 10

निमेषस्त्रिलवोः ज्ञेय आम्मातस्ते त्रयः क्षणः । क्षणान्संच विदुः काष्ठां लघुता दश पंच च ॥ लघूनि वै समाम्नाता दशपंच च नाडिका । ते द्वे मुहूर्तः प्रहरः षड्यामः सप्त वा नृणां !! द्वादशार्धपलोन्मानं चतुर्भिश्च तुरंगुलैः । स्वर्णमापैः कृतच्छिद्रंयावत्पास्थ जलप्लुतं ॥ यामाश्च त्वारश्च त्वारो मर्त्यानामहनी उभे । पक्षः पंचदशाहानि शुक्लः कृष्णश्च मानद । तयोस्समुच्चयो मासः पित्थणां तदहर्निशं । द्वौ तावृतुः षडयनं दक्षिणं चोत्तरं दिवि । अयने चाहनी प्रादुर्वत्सरो द्वादशस्स्कृतः । संवत्सरशतं नृणां परमायुर्निरूपितं ॥ (*nimeṣastrilavoḥ jñeya āmmātaste trayaḥ kṣaṇaḥ | kṣaṇānsaṃca viduḥ kāṣṭhāṃ laghutā daśa paṃca ca ‖ laghūni vai samāmnātā daśapaṃca ca nāḍikā | te dve muhūrtaḥ praharaḥ ṣaḍyāmaḥ sapta vā nṛṇāṃ !! dvādaśārdhapalonmānaṃ caturbhiśca turaṃgulaiḥ | svarṇamāpaiḥ kṛtacchidraṃyāvatpāstha jalaplutaṃ ‖ yāmāśca tvāraśca tvāro martyānāmahanī ubhe | pakṣaḥ paṃcadaśāhāni śuklaḥ kṛṣṇaśca mānada | tayossamuccayo māsaḥ pitthaṇāṃ tadaharniśaṃ | dvau tāvṛtuḥ ṣaḍayanaṃ dakṣiṇaṃ cottaraṃ divi | ayane cāhanī prādurvatsaro dvādaśasskṛtaḥ | saṃvatsaraśataṃ nṛṇāṃ paramāyurnirūpitaṃ ‖*) **GLOSS**

ग्रहर्षताराचक्रस्थः परमाण्वादिना जगत् । संवत्सरावसानेन पर्येत्यनिमिषो विभुः ॥ संवत्सरः परिवत्सर इडावत्सर एव च । अनुवत्सरो वत्सरश्च विदुरैवं प्रभाष्यते ॥ यः सृज्यशक्तिमुरुधोच्छासयॆन् स्वशक्ता पुंसोभ्रमाय दिवि धावति भूतभेदः । कालाख्यया गुणमयं क्रतुभिर्तन्वं स्वस्मै बलं हरत वत्सरपंचकाय ॥ (*graharṣatārācakrasthaḥ paramāṇvādinā jagat | saṃvatsarāvasānena paryetyanimiṣo vibhuḥ ‖ saṃvatsaraḥ parivatsara iḍāvatsara eva ca | anuvatsaro vatsaraśca viduraivaṃ prabhāṣyate ‖ yaḥ sṛjyaśaktimurudhocchāsayèn svaśaktā puṃsobhramāya divi dhāvati bhūtabhedaḥ | kālākhyayā guṇamayaṃ kratubhirtanvaṃ svasmai balaṃ harata vatsarapaṃcakāya ‖*) **GLOSS**

Vidura said:

पितृदेवमनुष्याणामायुः परमिदं स्मृतं । परेषां गतिमाचक्ष्य ये स्युः कल्पाद्बहिर्विदः ॥ भगवान् वेद कालस्य गतिं भगवतो ननु । विश्वं विचक्षते धीरा योगराद्धेन चक्षुषा ॥ (*pitṛdevamanuṣyāṇāmāyuḥ paramidaṃ smṛtaṃ | pareṣāṃ gatimācakṣya ye syuḥ kalpādbahirvidaḥ ‖ bhagavān veda kālasya gatiṃ bhagavato nanu | viśvaṃ vicakṣate dhīrā yogarāddhena cakṣuṣā ‖*) **GLOSS**

Maitreya said:

कृतु त्रेता द्वापरं च कलिश्चेति चतुर्युगं । दिव्यैर्द्वादशभिर्वर्षैः सावधानं निरूपितं ॥ (*kṛtu tretā dvāparaṃ ca kaliśceti caturyugaṃ | divyairdvādaśabhirvarṣaiḥ sāvadhānaṃ nirūpitaṃ ‖*) **GLOSS**

### Printed p. 11

चत्वारि त्रीणि द्वे चैकं कृतादिषु यथाक्रमं । संख्या तानि सहस्राणि द्विगुणानि शतानि च ॥ संध्यांशयोरंतरेण यः कालः शतसंख्ययोः । तमेवाहुर्युगं तज्ञा यत्र धर्मो विधीयते ॥ धर्मश्च तुष्पान्मनुजान् कृते समनुवर्तते । स एवान्येष्वधर्मेण व्यति पादेन वर्धता ॥ त्रिलोक्या युगसाहस्रं बहिराब्रह्मणो दिनं । तावत्येव निशा तात यन्निमीलति विश्वसृक् ॥ निशावसान आरब्बो लोककल्पोऽनुवर्तते । यावद्दिनं भगवतो मनून्फुंजंश्च तुर्दश ॥ स्वं स्वं कालं मनुर्भुंक्ते साधिकां ह्येकसप्ततिं । मन्वंतरेषु मनवस्तद्वंश्या ऋषयः सुराः ॥ भवंति चैव युगपत्सुरेशाश्चानु ये च तान् । एष दैनंदिनः सर्गो ब्राह्मस्त्रैलोक्यवर्तनः ॥ (*catvāri trīṇi dve caikaṃ kṛtādiṣu yathākramaṃ | saṃkhyā tāni sahasrāṇi dviguṇāni śatāni ca ‖ saṃdhyāṃśayoraṃtareṇa yaḥ kālaḥ śatasaṃkhyayoḥ | tamevāhuryugaṃ tajñā yatra dharmo vidhīyate ‖ dharmaśca tuṣpānmanujān kṛte samanuvartate | sa evānyeṣvadharmeṇa vyati pādena vardhatā ‖ trilokyā yugasāhasraṃ bahirābrahmaṇo dinaṃ | tāvatyeva niśā tāta yannimīlati viśvasṛk ‖ niśāvasāna ārabbo lokakalpo 'nuvartate | yāvaddinaṃ bhagavato manūnphuṃjaṃśca turdaśa ‖ svaṃ svaṃ kālaṃ manurbhuṃkte sādhikāṃ hyekasaptatiṃ | manvaṃtareṣu manavastadvaṃśyā ṛṣayaḥ surāḥ ‖ bhavaṃti caiva yugapatsureśāścānu ye ca tān | eṣa dainaṃdinaḥ sargo brāhmastrailokyavartanaḥ ‖*) **GLOSS**

तिर्यज् नृपितृदेवानां संभवो यत्र कर्मभिः । मन्वंतरेषु भगवान् बिभ्रत्सत्त्वं स्वमूर्तिभिः ॥ मन्यादिभिरिदं विश्वमवत्युदितपौरुषः तमोमात्रामुपादाय प्रतिसंरुद्धविक्रमः ॥ कालेनानुगताशेष आस्ते तूष्टीं दिनात्यये । तमेवान्वपिधीयंते लोका भूरादयस्त्रयः ॥ निशायामनुवृत्तायां निर्मुक्तशशिधास्करं । त्रिलोक्यां दह्यमानायां शक्त्या संकर्षणाग्निना ॥ यांत्यूष्मणा महर्लोकाज्जनं भृग्वादयोऽर्दिताः । तावत्रिभुवनं सद्यः कल्पांतैधितसिंधवः ॥ प्लावयंत्युत्कटाटोपचंडवातेरितोर्मयः । अंतः स तस्मिन् सलिल आस्तेऽनंतासनो हरिः ॥ (*tiryaj nṛpitṛdevānāṃ saṃbhavo yatra karmabhiḥ | manvaṃtareṣu bhagavān bibhratsattvaṃ svamūrtibhiḥ ‖ manyādibhiridaṃ viśvamavatyuditapauruṣaḥ tamomātrāmupādāya pratisaṃruddhavikramaḥ ‖ kālenānugatāśeṣa āste tūṣṭīṃ dinātyaye | tamevānvapidhīyaṃte lokā bhūrādayastrayaḥ ‖ niśāyāmanuvṛttāyāṃ nirmuktaśaśidhāskaraṃ | trilokyāṃ dahyamānāyāṃ śaktyā saṃkarṣaṇāgninā ‖ yāṃtyūṣmaṇā maharlokājjanaṃ bhṛgvādayo 'rditāḥ | tāvatribhuvanaṃ sadyaḥ kalpāṃtaidhitasiṃdhavaḥ ‖ plāvayaṃtyutkaṭāṭopacaṃḍavāteritormayaḥ | aṃtaḥ sa tasmin salila āste 'naṃtāsano hariḥ ‖*) **GLOSS**

### Printed p. 12

एवं विधैरहोरात्रॆ कालगत्योपलक्षितैः ॥ अपक्षितमिवास्यापि परमायुर्वयश्यतं । यदर्धमायुषस्तस्य पराधमभिधीयते ॥ पूर्वः पराधोपक्रांतो हप्यरोऽद्य प्रवर्तते । पूर्वस्यादौ पराधस्य ब्राह्मो नाममहानभूत् ॥ कल्पो यत्राभवद्ब्रह्मा शब्दब्रह्मेति यं विदुः । तस्यॆव चांते कल्पोऽभूद्यं पाद्ममभिचक्षते ॥ यद्धरेर्नाभिसरस आसील्लोकसरोरुहं । आयं तु कथितः कल्पो द्वितीयस्यापि भारत ॥ नाराह इति विख्यातो यत्रासीत्सूकरो हरिः । कालोऽयं द्विपराधाख्यो निमेष उपचर्यते ॥ अव्याकृतस्यानंतस्य अनादेर्जगदात्मनः । कालोऽयं परमाण्वादिर्द्विपराधांत ईश्वरः ॥ (*evaṃ vidhairahorātrè kālagatyopalakṣitaiḥ ‖ apakṣitamivāsyāpi paramāyurvayaśyataṃ | yadardhamāyuṣastasya parādhamabhidhīyate ‖ pūrvaḥ parādhopakrāṃto hapyaro 'dya pravartate | pūrvasyādau parādhasya brāhmo nāmamahānabhūt ‖ kalpo yatrābhavadbrahmā śabdabrahmeti yaṃ viduḥ | tasyèva cāṃte kalpo 'bhūdyaṃ pādmamabhicakṣate ‖ yaddharernābhisarasa āsīllokasaroruhaṃ | āyaṃ tu kathitaḥ kalpo dvitīyasyāpi bhārata ‖ nārāha iti vikhyāto yatrāsītsūkaro hariḥ | kālo 'yaṃ dviparādhākhyo nimeṣa upacaryate ‖ avyākṛtasyānaṃtasya anāderjagadātmanaḥ | kālo 'yaṃ paramāṇvādirdviparādhāṃta īśvaraḥ ‖*) **GLOSS**

नैवेशितुं प्रभुर्भूम्न ईश्वरो धाम मानिनां । विकारैः सहितो युक्तैर्विशेषादिभिरावृतः ॥ आंडकोशो बहिरयं पंचाशत्तोटिविस्तृतः । दशोत्तराधिकैरत्र प्रविष्टः परमाणुवत् ॥ लक्ष्यतेऽंतर्गताश्चान्ये कोटिशो ह्यंडराशयः । तदाहुरक्षरं ब्रह्म सर्वकारणकारणं ॥ विष्णोर्धाम परं साक्षात्पुरुषस्य महात्मानः । इति ते वर्णितः क्षत्तः कालाख्यः परमात्मनः ॥ महिमा वेदगर्भोथ यथास्राक्षिन्निबोध मे । (*naiveśituṃ prabhurbhūmna īśvaro dhāma mānināṃ | vikāraiḥ sahito yuktairviśeṣādibhirāvṛtaḥ ‖ āṃḍakośo bahirayaṃ paṃcāśattoṭivistṛtaḥ | daśottarādhikairatra praviṣṭaḥ paramāṇuvat ‖ lakṣyate 'ṃtargatāścānye koṭiśo hyaṃḍarāśayaḥ | tadāhurakṣaraṃ brahma sarvakāraṇakāraṇaṃ ‖ viṣṇordhāma paraṃ sākṣātpuruṣasya mahātmānaḥ | iti te varṇitaḥ kṣattaḥ kālākhyaḥ paramātmanaḥ ‖ mahimā vedagarbhotha yathāsrākṣinnibodha me |*) **GLOSS**

The measure of time is of two kinds, subtle and gross. For the extremely subtle, the usage is "atom" (paramāṇu); for the gross, "the supremely great" (parama-mahat).

### Printed p. 13

The exact measure of time follows the motion of the sun. Its divisions are as follows.

- 1 आणु (*āṇu*): an extremely small thing that cannot be divided
- 2 atoms (paramāṇu) = 1 द्वणुक (*dvaṇuka*)
- 3 ditto = 1 त्रसरेणु (*trasareṇu*)
- 3 त्रसरेणु (*trasareṇu*) = 1 त्रुटि (*truṭi*)
- 100 त्रुटि (*truṭi*) = 1 वेध (*vedha*)
- 3 वेध (*vedha*) = 1 लव (*lava*)
- 3 अवगळु (*avagaḷu*) = 1 निमेष (*nimeṣa*) **NOTE**
- 3 निमेष (*nimeṣa*) = 1 क्षण (*kṣaṇa*)
- 5 क्षण (*kṣaṇa*) = 1 काष्ठ (*kāṣṭha*)
- 15 काष्ठ (*kāṣṭha*) = 1 लघु (*laghu*)
- 15 लघु (*laghu*) = 1 नाडिका (*nāḍikā*)
- 2 नाडिका (*nāḍikā*) = 1 मुहूर्त (*muhūrta*)
- 6 or 7 नाडिका (*nāḍikā*) = 1 याम (*yāma*) or प्रहर (*prahara*) **NOTE**
- 8 याम (*yāma*) = 1 अहोरात्र (*ahorātra*)
- 15 अहोरात्र (*ahorātra*) = 1 पक्ष (*pakṣa*)
- 1 bright and 1 dark पक्ष (*pakṣa*) = 1 मास (*māsa*)
- 2 मास (*māsa*) = 1 ऋतु (*ṛtu*)
- 6 ditto = 1 अयन (*ayana*)
- 2 अयन (*ayana*) = 1 वत्सर (*vatsara*)
- 12 मास (*māsa*) = 1 संवत्सर (*saṃvatsara*)

(The varieties वत्सर (*vatsara*), संवत्सर (*saṃvatsara*), परिवत्सर (*parivatsara*), इडावत्सर (*iḍāvatsara*), अनुवत्सर (*anuvatsara*) and the rest follow the motions of the sun, the moon and the other planets.) One hundred संवत्सर (*saṃvatsara*) is the utmost limit of a man's life.

Satya (Kṛta), Tretā, Dvāpara and Kali are one cycle of the four yugas. That is, after the four yugas beginning with Kṛta have passed, the Kṛta yuga begins again. This order goes on, one following another. Together with their sandhyā and sandhyāṃśa they make 12000 years of the measure of the gods.

The beginning of a yuga is called sandhyā and the end of a yuga sandhyāṃśa. These are not included in the measures of the yugas. The measure of all of these together is as follows.

### Printed p. 14

The sandhyā of the Satya yuga is 400 years of the measure of the gods.

- Satya yuga: 4000 (years of the measure of the gods)
- its sandhyāṃśa: 400
- sandhyā of the Tretā yuga: 300
- Tretā yuga: 3000
- its sandhyāṃśa: 300
- sandhyā of the Dvāpara yuga: 200
- Dvāpara yuga: 2000
- its sandhyāṃśa: 200
- sandhyā of the Kali yuga: 100
- Kali yuga: 1000
- its sandhyāṃśa: 100

The total of the four yugas: .... .... 12000 years of the measure of the gods.

In the time that lies between the sandhyā and the sandhyāṃśa of a yuga, the practice of dharma is fixed. This dharma has 4 feet in the Satya yuga, 3 feet in the Tretā yuga, 2 feet in the Dvāpara, and 1 foot in the Kali.

Such a cycle of 1,000 yugas becomes one day of Brahmā. It may also be called a kalpa. That is, 12000 × 1000 = 1,20,00,000 years of the measure of the gods is the length of one day of Brahmā. So much time is the night of Brahmā. That is, a total of 24000000 years of the measure of the gods is one अहोरात्र (*ahorātra*) of Brahmā. In this one day 14 Manus wield their lordship. 1000/14 = 71 3/4: about 71 cycles of yugas. For 1 manvantara the total is 12000 × 1000/14 = 857146/7 years of the measure of the gods. **NOTE**

### Printed p. 15

In the creation of Brahmā, living beings, men, ancestors and gods are born according to their respective deeds.

In the manvantaras the Lord protects this world with the principle of sattva, both in person and, through Manu and the others, in the form of representatives. At the time of dissolution He gathers His power into Himself. The three worlds are burnt by the fire from the mouth of Saṅkarṣaṇa. The whole world is covered by water. In that water Hari, reclining on the coils of Ananta, remains in meditation.

The limit of the life of Brahmā too is 100 years. The half of this life is known as a parārdha. Now the first parārdha has passed and the second parārdha has begun. Each day of Brahmā is called a kalpa. The first part of this first parārdha is called Brahma-kalpa and its last part Padma-kalpa. The first part of the second parārdha, which is now going on, is called Varāha-kalpa. In this very kalpa the Varāha incarnation took place.

These two parārdhas of Brahmā taken together are for the Lord a mere nimeṣa (the time of the twinkling of an eye). He is not one who is measured by time. He is beyond time.

कालोऽयं द्विपरार्धाख्यो निमेष उपपद्यते । अव्याकृतस्यानंतस्य अनादेर्जगदात्मनः ॥ (*kālo 'yaṃ dviparārdhākhyo nimeṣa upapadyate | avyākṛtasyānaṃtasya anāderjagadātmanaḥ ‖*)
(Śrī. Bhā. 3-11-37)

In the Viṣṇu Purāṇa too the reckoning of the yugas beginning with Kṛta is the same as is explained in the Śrīmad Bhāgavata. But the measure of the manvantaras is stated somewhat more fully.

चतुरुगानां संख्याता साधिका ह्येकसप्ततिः (*caturugānāṃ saṃkhyātā sādhikā hyekasaptatiḥ*). In this manvantara there is a time that exceeds by seventy-one times the number of years in the four yugas. Besides, a slightly greater number is also added to it. In other Purāṇas too this additional number is indicated. But nowhere is it said how much its measure is.

In the Ṛgveda there is no explanation of any kind about the great divisions of time such as yuga, manvantara and kalpa mentioned above. But the use of this word is seen in the meanings (1) a pair, (2) one of the four divisions of time, Kṛta, Tretā and the rest, (3) a period of five years, (4) a yoke, (5) a measure or instrument of measuring, (6) a kind of plant used for medicine. For example—

### Printed p. 16

यद्वश्चित्रं युगेयुगे नव्यं घोषादमर्त्यं । (*yadvaścitraṃ yugeyuge navyaṃ ghoṣādamartyaṃ |*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-139-8) **GLOSS**

अश्वो न क्रंदञ्निभिः समिध्यते वैश्वानरः कुशिकेभिर्युगेयुगे । (*aśvo na kraṃdañnibhiḥ samidhyate vaiśvānaraḥ kuśikebhiryugeyuge |*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 3-26-3) **GLOSS**

युगेयुगे विदथ्यं गृणद्योऽग्ने रयिं यशसं धेहि नव्यसीं । (*yugeyuge vidathyaṃ gṛṇadyo 'gne rayiṃ yaśasaṃ dhehi navyasīṃ |*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 6-8-5) **GLOSS**

त्वां दूतमग्ने अमृतं युगेयुगे हव्यवाहं दधिरे पायुमीड्यं । (*tvāṃ dūtamagne amṛtaṃ yugeyuge havyavāhaṃ dadhire pāyumīḍyaṃ |*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 6-15-8) **GLOSS**

असो यथा नः शवसा चकानो युगेयुगे वयसा चेकितानः । (*aso yathā naḥ śavasā cakāno yugeyuge vayasā cekitānaḥ |*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 6-36-5) **GLOSS**

ध्रुवा एव वः पितरो युगेयुगे क्षेमकामासः सदसो न युंजते । (*dhruvā eva vaḥ pitaro yugeyuge kṣemakāmāsaḥ sadaso na yuṃjate |*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-94-12) **GLOSS**

For the words युगेयुगे (*yugeyuge*) in these Ṛks the bhāṣyakāra has explained the meaning as प्रतिदिनं (*pratidinaṃ*), कालेकाले (*kālekāle*), सर्वॆषु युगेषु (*sarvèṣu yugeṣu*): on every day, at every time, in all the yugas.

एतद्वचो जरितर्मापि मृष्टा आ यत्ते घोषानुत्तरा युगानि । (*etadvaco jaritarmāpi mṛṣṭā ā yatte ghoṣānuttarā yugāni |*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 3-33.8) **GLOSS**

आ घा ता गच्छानुत्तरा युगानि यत्र जामयः कृणवन्नजामि । (*ā ghā tā gacchānuttarā yugāni yatra jāmayaḥ kṛṇavannajāmi |*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-10-10) **GLOSS**

In these Ṛks the words उत्तरा युगानि (*uttarā yugāni*) have the meaning of the coming times, times that are to come (the future).

उक्तेषु शस्यमानेषु यः पश्यादुत्तरे युगे । (*ukteṣu śasyamāneṣu yaḥ paśyāduttare yuge |*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-72-1)

In this Ṛk the words उत्तरे युगे (*uttare yuge*) mean in the later yugas, that is, in the later yuga or time that comes after the earlier yuga has passed away.

पुरूणि रत्ना दधता न्यंस्मे अनु पूर्वाणि चख्यथुर्युगानि । (*purūṇi ratnā dadhatā nyaṃsme anu pūrvāṇi cakhyathuryugāni |*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 7-20-4) **GLOSS**

In this Ṛk the words पूर्वाणि युगानि (*pūrvāṇi yugāni*) mean in the yugas or times that have passed away in the past.

### Printed p. 17

वच्यंते वां ककुहा अप्पु जाता युगा जूर्णेव वरुणस्य भूरेः । (*vacyaṃte vāṃ kakuhā appu jātā yugā jūrṇeva varuṇasya bhūreḥ |*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-184-3) **GLOSS**

In this Ṛk the words युगा जूर्णॆ (*yugā jūrṇè*) mean the former or past time.

प्रमिनती मनुष्या युगानि योषा जारस्य चक्षसा वि भाति । (*praminatī manuṣyā yugāni yoṣā jārasya cakṣasā vi bhāti |*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-92-11) **GLOSS**

तदूचुषे मानुषेमा युगानि कीर्तेन्यं मघना नाम बिभ्रत् । (*tadūcuṣe mānuṣemā yugāni kīrtenyaṃ maghanā nāma bibhrat |*)
(Ṛ. Sa. 1-103-4) **NOTE** **GLOSS**

यत्रा नरो देवयंतो युगानि वितन्वते प्रति भद्राय भद्रं । (*yatrā naro devayaṃto yugāni vitanvate prati bhadrāya bhadraṃ |*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-115-2) **GLOSS**

अमिनती दैव्यानि व्रतानि प्रमिनती मनुष्या युगानि । (*aminatī daivyāni vratāni praminatī manuṣyā yugāni |*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-124-2) **GLOSS**

In these Ṛks, and in the Ṛks Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-144-4; 2-2-2; 5-52-4; 6-16-23; 7-9-4; 8-46-12; 8-51-9; 9-12-7; 10-27-19; 10-140-6, since the word मनुष्य (*manuṣya*) stands as an attribute of the words युगानि (*yugāni*), the meaning in all these Ṛks is the lifetime of men. That is, the limit of the life of men is indicated by the words मानुषा युगानि (*mānuṣā yugāni*).

In the Ṛk Ṛ. Saṃ. 5-73-3 also, the words नाहुषा युगा (*nāhuṣā yugā*) mean the lifetime of man. But—

दीर्घशतमा मामतेयो जुजुर्वान् दशमे युगे । (*dīrghaśatamā māmateyo jujurvān daśame yuge |*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 115-6) **NOTE** **GLOSS**

In explaining this Ṛk, Sāyaṇa has stated the view that the seer called Dīrghatamas, the son of the woman named Mamatā, by the grace of the Aśvin gods lived happily for ten yugas (दशयुगपर्यंतं (*daśayugaparyaṃtaṃ*)) with his wife, sons and others, and afterwards, that is after the ten yugas had passed, became old, with a worn-out body. Since it is not possible for a man to live for ten yugas, some Western scholars hold that the word yuga in this Ṛk may indicate a fixed period of five or ten years. According to this, it is natural that after ten yugas, that is after a hundred years, a man should be extremely old and have worn-out limbs; so runs their argument. However that may be, there is no difference of opinion here that the word yuga means a fixed time. In another sūkta of the Ṛgveda—

### Printed p. 18

या हिषधीः पूर्वा जाता देवेभ्यस्त्रियुगं पुरा । (*yā hiṣadhīḥ pūrvā jātā devebhyastriyugaṃ purā |*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-97-1) **GLOSS**

in this Ṛk it is said that the herbs and trees, that is the vegetable kingdom, were created three yugas before the creation of the gods. Here the word yuga may well be said to have the meaning of the greatest division of time. For since the vegetable kingdom is the chief food of the other moving creatures, of the gods and of men, it is reasonable to say that the vegetable kingdom was created long before the gods. Modern science too supports this.

As we have said before, the names of the four yugas, Kṛta, Tretā, Dvāpara and Kali, as set out in that order, are not found anywhere in the Ṛgveda. But in the Aitareya Brāhmaṇa, in one place only,

कलिः शयानो भवति संजिहानस्तु द्वापरः । उत्तिष्ठंस्त्रेता भवति कृतं संपद्यदे चरंश्चरैवेति ॥ (*kaliḥ śayāno bhavati saṃjihānastu dvāparaḥ | uttiṣṭhaṃstretā bhavati kṛtaṃ saṃpadyade caraṃścaraiveti ‖*)
(Ai. Brā. 33-15)

the names of the four yugas, Kali, Dvāpara, Tretā and Kṛta, are indicated. These four names are compared to the four states of a man. It is said that when a man lies asleep he is in the state called Kali, when awake in Dvāpara, when he has risen and stands in Tretā, and when he moves about in the state called Kṛta; therefore the yugas Kali, Dvāpara, Tretā and Kṛta are each in order better than the one before. Some think that the words Kali and the rest denote the movements of the dice used in the game of dice.

#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> ब्रह्म॑ण॒स्पति॑रे॒ता सं क॒र्मार॑ इवाधमत् ।  
> दे॒वानां॑ पू॒र्व्ये यु॒गेऽस॑तः॒ सद॑जायत ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> ब्रह्मणः । पतिः । एता । सं । कर्मारः इव । अधमत् ।  
> देवानां । पूर्व्ये । युगे । असतः । सत् । आजायत ॥ २ ॥

### Printed p. 19

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> ब्रह्मणोऽन्नस्य पतिरदितिरेतैतानि देवानां जन्मानि कर्मार इव स यथा
> भस्त्रयाग्निमुपधमिति प्रज्वलनार्थमेवं समधमत् । उदपादयदित्यर्थः । देवानां
> पूर्व्य युगे । आदिसृष्टावित्यर्थः । तेषामुपादानकारणादसतो नामरूपवर्जितत्वेनासत्समानाद्ब्रह्मणः सकाशात्सन्नामरूपविशिष्टं देवादिकमजायत । प्रादुरभूत् । असद्वा इदमग्र आसीत्ततो वै सदजायत । तै.उ. २-७ । इति हि श्रुतिः । न
> सदात्मकस्य प्रपंचस्यासत्कारणत्वं युक्तमिति वाच्यं छंदोगैः कथमसतः सज्जायेतेत्यसत्कारणत्वमाक्षिप्त्य सत्येव सोम्येदमग्र आसीदित्यवधारितत्वात् । छां.उ.
> ६-२-२ । तर्ह्यसत्कारणप्रतिपादकवाक्यानां का गतिरिति चेत् तेषामव्याकृतत्वाभिप्रायत्वात् तद्धेदं तर्ह्यव्याकृतमासीत् । शत. १४-४-२-१५ । इति श्रुतेः । यद्येवं
> तर्ह्यदितेः सकाशात्कथं देवाद्युत्पत्तिः । वायोरग्निरित्यादिवत् अधिष्ठानसकाशादुत्पत्तेः । यद्वा । देवानां कारणभूतं सदसतो ब्रह्मणः सकाशादुत्पन्नमिति योज
> नादुक्तन्यायोऽस्मिन्पक्षेऽपि समान एव ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa says that Brahmaṇaspati is Aditi, the lord of food (ब्रह्मणः (*brahmaṇaḥ*) being food). As a smith blows up a fire with his bellows to make it blaze, so she blew together, that is, brought forth, these births of the gods. देवानां पूर्व्य युगे (*devānāṃ pūrvya yuge*) means in the first creation. From their material cause, the asat, that is Brahman, which because it is without name and form is like non-being, the sat, the gods and the rest endowed with name and form, was born, appeared. For the Śruti says असद्वा इदमग्र आसीत्ततो वै सदजायत (*asadvā idamagra āsīttato vai sadajāyata*) (Tai. U. 2-7). It is not to be objected that it is unfitting for a world of the nature of sat to have asat as its cause, for the followers of the Chāndogya, after raising the objection कथमसतः सज्जायेत (*kathamasataḥ sajjāyeta*) and thus questioning asat as cause, settle the matter by सत्येव सोम्येदमग्र आसीत् (*satyeva somyedamagra āsīt*) (Chā. U. 6-2-2). If it is asked what then is the way of the sentences that teach asat as cause, the answer is that they intend the unmanifest state, as the Śruti says तद्धेदं तर्ह्यव्याकृतमासीत् (*taddhedaṃ tarhyavyākṛtamāsīt*) (Śata. 14-4-2-15). **NOTE** If so, how is there origin of the gods and the rest from Aditi? Because, as in वायोरग्निः (*vāyoragniḥ*) and the like, origin is from the substratum. Or else: it is to be construed that what is the cause of the gods arose from Brahman, who is both sat and asat; the reasoning stated above holds equally on this view.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- कर्मार इव (*karmāra iva*)—as a blacksmith presses his bellows and makes the fire grow
- ब्रह्मणस्पतिः (*brahmaṇaspatiḥ*)—Brahmaṇaspati (Aditi, the lord of food)
- एता (*etā*)—these gods
- समधमत् (*samadhamat*)—filled with vitality and made grow
- देवानां (*devānāṃ*)—the gods
- पूर्वे युगे (*pūrve yuge*)—in the time that precedes their coming to be
- असतः (*asataḥ*)—from the principle that is without name and form, unembodied
- सत् (*sat*)—the gods and the rest endowed with names and forms
- अजायत (*ajāyata*)—were born

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

As a blacksmith presses his bellows and makes the fire grow, Brahmaṇaspati filled these gods with vitality and made them grow. In the time that precedes the coming to be of the gods, the gods and the rest, endowed with names and forms, were born from the unembodied principle that is without name and form.

##### English translation
Brahmanaspati filled these (generations of the gods) with breath as a blacksmith (his bellows); in the first age of the gods the existent was born of the nonexistent.

### Printed p. 20

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

Two kinds of meaning may be given here for the word Brahmaṇaspati. Brahmaṇaspati means Prajāpati, the protector of food, or the Lord of all (Sarveśvara), or Aditi. Aditi also means the power that governs the universe. We shall explain this matter at length in the Special Topics of the fourth Ṛk of this sūkta.

ब्रह्मणस्पतिः एताः कर्मार इव अधमत् (*brahmaṇaspatiḥ etāḥ karmāra iva adhamat*)—As a blacksmith, who works in iron, to heat the iron presses the fire (the live coals) with his bellows, makes much air come, and so makes the fire blaze, so the Lord of all filled the gods with His unobstructed power and made them endowed with vitality. All kinds of powers are in the Lord of all. The idea is that He established a part of those powers in the gods and made them possessed of vitality. The vitality that is in the Supreme Self is like an ocean. As the ocean is the original source of all the bodies of water on the earth, rivers, wells, tanks and the rest, so all the vitality that is in the gods and in other living beings is to be understood as a part of the vitality of that Supreme Self or Lord of all. Therefore the main idea of this sentence is that at the time of creation the Lord of all filled a part of the vitality that is His own portion into the gods and the rest, and made them gods who are not inert, who can move about, can think, and have the power of action.

देवानां पूर्वे युगॆ असतः सत् अजायत (*devānāṃ pūrve yugè asataḥ sat ajāyata*)—Here देवानां पूर्वे युगे (*devānāṃ pūrve yuge*) means before Prajāpati (or the Lord of all) created the gods, in the first creation. At that time, that is before creation, there were no things at all endowed with names and forms (that is, mountains, rivers, trees, animals, men and the like). There was only the unmanifest form. That is, the Supreme Self, by Himself alone, endowed with His infinite power that is beyond the reach of the senses, pervaded everywhere. This form of the Supreme Self has names such as unmanifest, without qualities and asat. That is what is indicated here by the word असत् (*asat*). In this sentence the seer describes the order of creation: from this unmanifest form, sat, that is, mountains and the rest and living beings and the like that are perceptible to the senses, came into being. This matter is supported at length by the Śruti sentence आसद्वा इदमग्र आसीत् । ततो वै सदजायत (*āsadvā idamagra āsīt | tato vai sadajāyata*) (Tai. U. 2-7) (the Taittirīya Upaniṣad), by the Chāndogya Upaniṣad sentences कथमसतः सज्जायेत। (*kathamasataḥ sajjāyeta|*) and सत्येव सोम्येदमग्र आसीत् (*satyeva somyedamagra āsīt*) (Chā. U. 6-2-2), by the Śatapatha Brāhmaṇa sentence तद्देदं तर्हव्याकृतमासीत् (*taddedaṃ tarhavyākṛtamāsīt*) (Śa. Brā. 14-2-15), by the Taittirīya Āraṇyaka sentence वायोरग्निः (*vāyoragniḥ*) (Tai. Ā. 8-1), and by the Ṛgveda sentence नासदासीन्नो सदासीत् (*nāsadāsīnno sadāsīt*) (10-129-1). This last sentence in the Ṛgveda we shall explain at length later, in its own context.

### Printed p. 21
#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> दे॒वानां॑ यु॒गे प्र॑थ॒मेऽस॑तः॒ सद॑जायत ।  
> तदाशा॒ अन्व॑जायंत॒ तदु॑त्ता॒नप॑द॒स्परि॑ ॥ ३ ।

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> देवानां । युगे प्रथमे । असतः । सत् । अजायत ।  
> तत् । आशाः । अनु । अजायंत । तत् । उत्तानपदः । परिः ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> पूर्वार्धमुक्तं । तदन्वाशा दिशोऽजायंत । तत्परि । तदन्वित्यर्थः । उत्तानपदः । उत्तानमूर्ध्वतानं पद्यंत इत्युत्तानपदो वृक्षाः । तेऽजायंत । प्रादुरभवन् ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa says that the first half of the Ṛk has already been explained. After that, the quarters (the directions) were born. The word तत्परि (*tatpari*) means "after that". उत्तानपदः (*uttānapadaḥ*) are those that stretch upward and spread (as if by their feet); these are the trees. They were born, that is, they came into manifestation.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- देवानां (*devānāṃ*)—of the gods, who are born
- प्रथमे युगे (*prathame yuge*)—in the first age, the primal time of creation
- असतः (*asataḥ*)—from the principle devoid of name and form
- सत् (*sat*)—the principle of the world, endowed with name and form
- अजायत (*ajāyata*)—was born
- तत् अनु (*tat anu*)—after that
- आशाः (*āśāḥ*)—the quarters
- अजायंत (*ajāyaṃta*)—were born
- तत्परि (*tatpari*)—after that
- उत्तानपदः (*uttānapadaḥ*)—the trees and the like, which grow upward, were born

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

In the primal time when the gods are born (the time of creation), from the principle devoid of name and form, the principle of the world, endowed with name and form, was born. After it the quarters were born. After that the trees and the like, which grow upward, were born.

##### English translation
In the first age of the gods the existent was born of the non-existent; after that the quarters (of the horizon) were born, and after them the upward-growing (trees).

### Printed p. 22

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

The latter half of the previous Ṛk is recited here as the first half of this Ṛk. But in place of the word पूर्वे (*pūrve*) the word प्रथमे (*prathame*) is used. There is no difference in meaning. Yet, when examined closely, there is a slight difference after all. प्रथमे युगे (*prathame yuge*) means: the very first age, when creation begins, may be called the first age. The former age, however, would have to be spoken of as one among the earlier ages, other than the age that is now running. For it is said that the creation of the plant kingdom took place three ages before the creation of the gods, in the Ṛk या ओषधीः पूर्व जाता देवेभ्यस्त्रियुगं पुरा (*yā oṣadhīḥ pūrva jātā devebhyastriyugaṃ purā*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-97-1); and in the latter half of this Ṛk also, in तदुत्तानपदस्परि (*taduttānapadaspari*), it is said that the trees and the like (herbs and trees, that is, the plant kingdom) were born after the first age and before the creation of the gods. Since the plant kingdom is the chief food of the gods, of men and of all other kinds of living beings, it is reasonable and fitting to say that the plant kingdom (trees and the like) was created before the creation of living beings.

#### Ṛk 4

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> भूर्ज॑ज्ञ उत्ता॒नप॑दो भु॒व आशा॑ अजायंत ।  
> अदि॑ते॒र्दक्षो॑ अजायत॒ दक्षा॒द्वदि॑तिः॒ परिः॑ ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> भूः । जज्ञे । उत्तान्यपदः । भुवः । आशाः । अजायंत ।  
> अदितेः । दक्षः । अजायत । दक्षात् । ऊं इति । अदितिः । परि ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> भूरुत्तानपदो वृक्षाज्जज्ञे । तथा भुवः सकाशादाशा अजायंत । तथादितेर्दक्षोऽजायत । उत्पन्नः । दक्षादु दक्षादप्यदितिः पर्यजायत । न स्वोत्पन्नं
> कार्यं स्वस्यॆव कारणमपि भवतीति विप्रतिषिद्धमिति वाच्यं । यास्काचार्य इदमेव वाक्यमुदाहृत्य विरोधमाशंक्य पर्यहरत् । तथा हि । आदितेर्दक्षो अजायत दक्षाद्वदितिः परीति च तत्कथमुपपद्येत समानजन्मानौ स्यातामित्यपि ना देवधर्मेणेतरेतरजन्मानौ स्यातामितरेतरप्रकृती । नि. ११-२३ । इति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa says: the earth was born from the उत्तानपदः (*uttānapadaḥ*), the tree. Likewise the quarters were born from the earth. Likewise Dakṣa was born, that is, came into being, from Aditi; and from Dakṣa, in turn, Aditi too was born. It should not be objected that this is contradictory, since a product that has been born of something would here also become the cause of that very thing. Ācārya Yāska quoted this very sentence, raised the doubt of contradiction, and answered it (Ni. 11-23): how can this be, since "from Aditi Dakṣa was born, from Dakṣa Aditi" would make the two of one and the same birth? Or else, by the nature of the gods, the two may be born of each other, each being the origin of the other.

### Printed p. 23

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- भूः (*bhūḥ*)—the world of the earth
- उत्तानपदः (*uttānapadaḥ*)—from the tree
- जज्ञे (*jajñe*)—was born
- भुवः (*bhuvaḥ*)—from the earth
- आशाः (*āśāḥ*)—the quarters
- अजायंत (*ajāyaṃta*)—were born
- अदितेः (*aditeḥ*)—from Aditi
- दक्षः (*dakṣaḥ*)—Dakṣa
- अजायत (*ajāyata*)—was born
- दक्षादु (*dakṣādu*)—again from Dakṣa
- अदितिः (*aditiḥ*)—Aditi
- पर्यजायत (*paryajāyata*)—was born

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

From the tree the world of the earth was born. From the earth the quarters were born. From Aditi Dakṣa was born. Again from Dakṣa Aditi was born.

##### English translation
The earth was born from the upward growing (tree) the quarters, were born from the earth ; Daksha was born from Aditi, and afterwards Aditi from Daksha

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

In the first half of this Ṛk it is said that from the trees the earth was born, and from the earth the quarters. Some explanation is necessary on this point. Everyone knows that the plant kingdom and trees and the like are on the earth. Since the earth is necessary for their birth, the earth must have existed before the trees and the like. But here it is said that the earth was born from the trees; to say that the trees existed first and the earth came into being afterwards is mutually contradictory, is it not? The answer is this: before the earth, water was created, and that water was spread everywhere. The Śruti sentences आपो वा इदमग्रे सलिलमासीत् (*āpo vā idamagre salilamāsīt*) and आपो वा इदं सर्वं विश्वा भूतान्यापः (*āpo vā idaṃ sarvaṃ viśvā bhūtānyāpaḥ*), and the Smṛti sentence अप एव ससर्जाद् (*apa eva sasarjād*), also establish that Prajāpati first, that is, before creating the earth, created water. Besides, the Upaniṣad sentence अग्नेरापः । अद्भः पृथिवी (*agnerāpaḥ | adbhaḥ pṛthivī*) also says that the earth came into being from water. Therefore water is the origin of the earth. Is there a birth of plants and the like in water? One may say that there is. That various kinds of plants exist in the lower part of the sea is a matter known to all. Or else the word उत्तानपद (*uttānapada*) here may be taken to mean "growing or spreading upward". That a solid mass like the earth arises, in the mass of the sea water, from a kind of living creature that grows upward (coral) is well known to modern scientists. Or else it may be said, if one wishes, that the earth, made solid from the water that is of the nature of sap, came into being.

### Printed p. 24
As for the matter of the quarters being born from the earth, only this much can be said. At the time of creation, that is, before the earth was created, when water covered everything everywhere and there were no divisions such as above and below, an arrangement of the quarters was not possible. After the earth was created, the arrangement of the quarters, east, west, south, north, above and below, came about of itself; that is, the need for it arose. Therefore it is said here that the quarters were born from the earth.
In the latter half of this Ṛk, in अदितिर्दक्षो अजायत दक्षाद्वदितिः परि (*aditirdakṣo ajāyata dakṣādvaditiḥ pari*), there are two sentences. That Dakṣa was born from Aditi is the first sentence; that Aditi was born from Dakṣa is the second sentence. Is not the meaning (the matter) in these sentences mutually contradictory? That an effect arises from a cause is right; but how can an effect become the origin of the cause? How is this apparent contradiction resolved? The answer may be given in two or three ways. Thus: the word Aditi in the first sentence may be taken to mean the power that governs the universe, or the Supreme Brahman. Then it becomes consistent that from the Supreme Brahman Dakṣa was born, and from Dakṣa his daughter Aditi was born. Or, as Yāska has said—
आदित्यो दक्ष इत्याहुरादित्यमध्ये च स्तुतः । अदितिदर्दाक्षायणी । आदितेर्दक्षो अजायत दक्षाद्वदितिः परि । इति च । तत्कथमुपपद्येत समानजन्मानौ स्यातामिति । अपि वा देवधर्मेणेतरेतरजन्मानौ स्यातामितरेतरप्रकृती ॥ (*ādityo dakṣa ityāhurādityamadhye ca stutaḥ | aditidardākṣāyaṇī | āditerdakṣo ajāyata dakṣādvaditiḥ pari | iti ca | tatkathamupapadyeta samānajanmānau syātāmiti | api vā devadharmeṇetaretarajanmānau syātāmitaretaraprakṛtī ‖*)
(Ni. 11-23)
that is, Āditya too is called Dakṣa. This Dakṣa is praised among the sons of Aditi, that is, reckoned as one of Aditi's sons. Aditi is called Dākṣāyaṇī, that is, daughter of Dakṣa. Now, in the sentence अदितेर्दक्षो अजायत दक्षाद्वदितिः परि (*aditerdakṣo ajāyata dakṣādvaditiḥ pari*), in what is said there, that Dakṣa and Aditi were born from Aditi and from Dakṣa respectively, does a mutual contradiction not appear? How is this possible? The answer: it should be understood that the seer describes in this way the fact that the place of origin of both Dakṣa and Aditi is one and the same. Besides, since among the gods one is seen to be born from another, or their natures and the like are seen to follow one another, the description is made thus; there is a custom of calling Agni Aditi; so Yāska has given the explanation of this Ṛk. **NOTE**
In Ṛ. Saṃ. 2-27-1 it is said that Aditi is the mother of the Āditya named Dakṣa. Again, at two places, in the Ṛks 10-5-7 and 10.64-5, the mutual relation of Dakṣa and Aditi is quite peculiar,

### Printed p. 25
In explaining the word Aditi, Yāska, having explained the word Aditi as "Aditi is the undecaying mother of the gods" (Ni. 4-22), that is, one of unwasting power, the mother of the gods, quoted the Ṛk that makes known the nature of Aditi and her many forms—
अदितिदौर्योदितिरंतरिक्षमवितिर्माता स पिता स पुत्रः । विश्वे देवा अदितिः पंचजना अदितिर्जातमदितिर्जनित्वं ॥ (*aditidauryoditiraṃtarikṣamavitirmātā sa pitā sa putraḥ | viśve devā aditiḥ paṃcajanā aditirjātamaditirjanitvaṃ ‖*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-89-10)
In this Ṛk the undivided Aditi is described as the giver of birth to the whole universe; she is the mother of the world and the father of the world. She herself is every thing that is born; Aditi is the place of origin of all. She herself is the five kinds of peoples (the four castes together with the Niṣādas and the rest); Aditi is all the gods; she is the shining heaven, she is the atmosphere, she is the whole universe, she is the governor of the universe. **NOTE**
Apart from the two deities Niṣṭigrī and Uṣas, among those who are directly designated mother of the gods, Aditi is known to be the first deity.
**1. The special features describing the nature of Aditi.**
Although there is no separate sūkta addressed to Aditi, Aditi is praised and prayed to by the mantras contained in many sūktas. She is prayed to bestow sons and the like, and wealth such as cattle. Her protection and her forgiveness are asked for in many places (1-43-2). She is described also as the mother of Varuṇa and of some other deities. In the Nighaṇṭu the word Aditi is included and recited among (1) the names of the earth, (2) the names of speech, (3) the names of the cow, and (4) the names of heaven and earth (Ni. 1- ; 1-11 2-11; 3-30). **NOTE**. In the Nirukta, in section 4-22, Aditi is described as "undecaying mother of the gods", the mother of the gods of great power.
In the Ṛgveda Saṃhitā (1-113-19) Uṣas, the mother of the gods, is described as the host of Aditi **NOTE** (Sāyaṇa has given a somewhat different meaning). Here the gross meaning is "the face of Aditi". It may also be said that she is another form of hers. In another part of the Nirukta (11-22), following the classification of the deities (as shown above), where the deities of the atmosphere are explained, the first place among the female deities is given to Aditi.
अथातो मध्यस्थानाः । शासां अदितिः प्रथमागामिनी भवति (*athāto madhyasthānāḥ | śāsāṃ aditiḥ prathamāgāminī bhavati*)
(3. 11-22) **NOTE**

### Printed p. 26
Among the female deities of the middle world, the first place belongs to Aditi.
In the Ṛgveda Saṃhitā, in the Ṛks 4-55-3; 4-55-7; 5-51.11; 6-50-1; 7-38-4; 7-40-2; 8-25-10; 8-27-5; 8-56-10 and so on, Aditi is described as a goddess; in the mantras 2-40-6; 7-40-4; 10-92-14, as देवी आदितिरनर्वा (*devī āditiranarvā*), Aditi of unobstructed power and divine nature; in 1-136-3 as radiant, as the bearer of the earth, and as dwelling in the heavenly world (ज्योतिष्मतीमदितिं (*jyotiṣmatīmaditiṃ*) ...). Besides, the epithets उरुव्यचाः (*uruvyacāḥ*), one who pervades most widely (5-46-6); विश्वजन्यां (*viśvajanyāṃ*), a dear friend to all men (7-10-4); and the like are noteworthy. Praising such an excellent Aditi, benefactress of the world,
"In the morning, at midday and in the evening, I pray to and call upon Aditi continuously" — so the seer praises and prays (5-69-3).
In the mantra 1.185-3 a gift of wealth from that same Aditi, pure, undecaying, divine and most venerable, is asked for. In 1-166-12, in praising the Maruts, Aditi is praised by saying that the favour to be given by them is equal to the gift of Aditi. In 4-55-3 पस्त्या (*pastyā*) is an epithet indicating a special quality of Aditi. From this it is known that she is the household deity who gives shelter to all. In the Vājasaneyi Saṃhitā the great qualities of Aditi are praised thus—
महीमूषु पातरं सुप्रतानां........... अदितिं सुप्रणीतिं । (*mahīmūṣu pātaraṃ supratānāṃ........... aditiṃ supraṇītiṃ |*)
(Tai. Saṃ. 1-5-11-5 and Vā. Saṃ. 21-5)
her greatness is described thus: let us pray to and call upon Aditi, who is of the nature of a mother giving shelter to devotees, the leader of the sacrifice, possessed of surpassing power, free from old age, spread most widely, a protector, who skilfully shows the way to all and leads all on the good path.
**2. The opinions stated by Roth and Max-muller concerning the original form of Aditi:**
Aditi, in the opinion of the scholar Roth, is a synonym giving the meaning "the infinite", for describing the infinity of the atmosphere, which possesses a greatness exceeding that of the earth with its fixed boundaries, and has an immeasurable extent

### Printed p. 27
This is how it stands. The same scholar expressed the same opinion when explaining a sentence of the Nirukta. For the word Aditi the meanings "unassailable, not to be transgressed, undecaying, beyond measure" come naturally. "She is a special deity reflecting infinity. It is in this form that she is worshipped. Just as the supernatural divine radiance is regarded as an unearthly and eternal principle, so also the Ādityas, Varuṇa, Mitra, Aramā, Bhaga and the rest, who are of the nature of light, are regarded as sons of this eternal principle, which has the form of Aditi. Because she reflects the eternal principle that is beyond measure and beyond worldly perception, it has not been possible in the Vedic literature for a definite form and so on of this deity to be described. This eternal principle, which bears the Ādityas and is itself borne by the Ādityas, is the support of all the Ādityas. This same divine light is also the essence of them all." Thus the same scholar has stated his opinion.
(Journal of the German Oriental Society Vol. 6. 68)
"The deity Aditi, who reflects the principle called the Infinite, is the most ancient of all the deities seen by the seers. But she is not a principle conceived in abstract form in purely mental imagination; she is a deity who includes the universe in concrete form. She is perceptible to human sight, of infinite extent, surpassing the earth, and beyond the worlds such as the atmosphere. It is because she reflects infinity that Aditi is described by many kinds of epithets."
(Translation of the Rig-veda, 1-230)
**1. Passages describing Aditi as the mother of the Ādityas:—** **NOTE**
From the sentences quoted below it is known that Aditi is the mother of Varuṇa and the other deities. Not only the sentences of the Ṛgveda Saṃhitā but other Śrutis also state this.
"So that Mitra and Varuṇa, lords of all riches, may display their divine power, Aditi, mother of the world, of great nature, worthy of worship, gave birth to Mitra and Varuṇa"
(8-25-3)
"May Aditi, mother of the wealthy Mitra, of Aryaman and of the pure-souled Varuṇa, grant us protection."
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-36-3; 10-132 6 ; A. Ve. 5-1-9) (8-47-9) **NOTE**
In 2-67-7 she is designated राजपुत्रा (*rājaputrā*), that is, one whose sons are the Ādityas, Varuṇa and the rest, lords of the world; in 3-4-11 सुपुत्रा (*suputrā*), one who has excellent sons; in 8-56-11 उग्रपुत्रा (*ugraputrā*), one who has very

### Printed p. 28
mighty sons; and in the Atharvaveda, in the mantras 3.8-2 and 11-11-1, शूरपुत्रा (*śūraputrā*). In the mantra 8-9-21 of the same (A. Ne. **NOTE**) she is praised as अष्टयोनिरदितिरष्टपुत्रा (*aṣṭayoniraditiraṣṭaputrā*), Aditi who has eight wombs and eight sons.
In the mantra 8-90-15 of the same Atharvaveda the relation between the Ādityas and Aditi is stated somewhat differently.
माता रुद्राणां दुहिता वसूनां स्वसादित्यानां..................आदितिं वधिष्ठ (*mātā rudrāṇāṃ duhitā vasūnāṃ svasādityānāṃ..................āditiṃ vadhiṣṭha*)
Aditi is described as the daughter of the Vasus, the mother of the Rudras and the sister of the Ādityas. In the Sāmaveda also the brothers and the sons of Aditi are separately designated, as stated in the sentence quoted below.
त्वष्टा नो दैव्यं वचः..................त्रामणं वचः (*tvaṣṭā no daivyaṃ vacaḥ..................trāmaṇaṃ vacaḥ*)
(Sā. Pe- 129) **NOTE**
May Tvaṣṭṛ, Parjanya and Brahmaṇaspati recite blessings for us. May Aditi also, with her sons and her brothers (पुत्रैर्भ्रातृभिः (*putrairbhrātṛbhiḥ*)), recite words of protection for us.
**4. Is there identity between Aditi and the atmosphere?**
In the Ṛk 10-63.2 Aditi is described, together with water and the earth, as a source of the gods. The meaning of that mantra is: "O gods, all your names are sacred and worthy of worship. You who are born in the womb of Aditi, in the womb of the earth, and अब्बरू (*abbarū*) **NOTE**, hear our prayer."
[This division of the gods is threefold: those dwelling in heaven, those dwelling in the atmosphere and those dwelling on the earth. (We discussed this order of division earlier, when we quoted the mantra 1-139-11.) It is a special feature that in place of "dwelling in the atmosphere" the expression "born of the waters" is used. But this usage occurs not only in this context, but also in 10-49-2. "All the gods, born of heaven, born of the atmosphere and born of the earth, worship me alone as Indra", thus runs this division. (In both these examples the word अप् (*ap*) may be explained as the atmosphere.) In the same way the same divisions are found precisely in the Ṛks 18-65-9; 2-38-11; 10-45-1 and 7-35-11 **NOTE**. But in the mantras 6-50-11 and 7-35-14 there are four divisions: दिव्याः (*divyāḥ*), पार्थिवासः (*pārthivāsaḥ*), गोजाताः (*gojātāḥ*) and अप्याः (*apyāḥ*) (heavenly, earthly, born of cows and aquatic).]

### Printed p. 29
As in 10-63-2, this point can be observed also in the Ṛk 10-63-3. "Offer praises to the Ādityas, givers of rain and doers of good deeds, to whom the earth, their mother, gives milk full of sweetness, and the deity of the heavenly world, in the form of Aditi, gives nectar." Here the word अदितः (*aditaḥ*) is an epithet of द्यौः (*dyauḥ*). Besides, in the Ṛk 5-59-8 also the usage मिमातु द्यौरदितिर्वीतये (*mimātu dyauraditirvītaye*) occurs. Here Sāyaṇa himself has not given a precise meaning for the word Aditi. He has given one meaning: द्यौः अंतरिक्षं ; कीदृशी । अदितिः अदीना । (*dyauḥ aṃtarikṣaṃ ; kīdṛśī | aditiḥ adīnā |*), that is, द्यौः (*dyauḥ*) is the atmosphere, and Aditi is the undecaying one; and secondly, with अदितिःभूमिश्च (*aditiḥbhūmiśca*), he has given for the word Aditi another meaning, the earth. Such contexts show the identity of Aditi with the atmosphere. But in the mantra
देवा आदित्या आदितिं हवामहे ये पार्थिवासो दिव्यासो अप्पुये । (*devā ādityā āditiṃ havāmahe ye pārthivāso divyāso appuye |*)
(10-65-9)
those born of the earth and those born of the heavenly world are mentioned separately, besides the Ādityas and their mother Aditi; in this context it is stated that Aditi and the heavenly world are different.
**5. Evidence that Aditi is a deity different from the earth.**
Although in some of the sentences quoted above identity of Aditi with the atmosphere is stated, it cannot be said that the same relation of identity is shown in the other parts of the Saṃhitā. At times there are contexts that show the earth and Aditi to be one. But there are also descriptions that differ from this. It has been stated above that, according to the readings of the Nirukta, the word is at one time a synonym of the earth and at another of heaven and earth. In one or two contexts the word Aditi is an epithet of the earth, as in "the earth, in the form of Aditi, together with sons who perform excellent rites, stands by the favour of Agni" and so on (1. 72-9) (A. Ve. 13-1-38). But in many other contexts it is known precisely that she is a deity different from the earth. For example,
"May heaven and earth, the waters, and the sun together with the stars hear our prayer"
thus it is prayed in the mantra 3-54-19, and then, in what follows it, that is, in the Ṛk 3-54-20,
"May Aditi, together with the Ādityas, hear our prayer"
it is prayed. Therefore there is no doubt that Aditi is different from the earth. If we take a few more examples,
"I pray to and call upon Indra, Agni, Mitra, Varuṇa, Aditi, the atmosphere, the earth,

### Printed p. 30
heaven, the Maruts, the mountains and the waters" (5-46-3)
"O heaven, in the form of the father, O earth, the benefactress, O Agni, in the form of a brother, O Vasus, be givers of happiness to us. O Ādityas, O goddess Aditi, give us complete protection"
(6-51-5)
"May Mitra, Varuṇa, Aditi, the presiding deity of the ocean, the earth and heaven be givers of joy to us" (9-97-58)
"May heaven and earth, wise and discerning, protect us. May Aditi, the mother of Mitra and Varuṇa, protect us from calamities." (10-36-2,3)
"Heaven and earth, Yama, Aditi, Tvaṣṭṛ ... Viṣṇu are worthy of our worship" (18-92-11) **NOTE**
"We shall take refuge in the earth that gives excellent protection, in heaven free from sin, in Aditi the guide ..." (10-63.-10)
In such sentences the difference between the earth and Aditi is clear. In support of the same opinion there is in the Vājasaneyi Saṃhitā also this prayer: "पृथिवी चमेऽदितिश्च मे (*pṛthivī came 'ditiśca me*) .... may the earth, Aditi and Diti .... satisfy me" [Vā. Saṃ. 18-22]. In the Atharvaveda also, भूमिर्माता अदितिर्नो जनित्रं (*bhūmirmātā aditirno janitraṃ*) ........, it is said: may the earth, in the form of mother, and Aditi, the cause of birth, protect us (Ā. Ve. 6-120-2) **NOTE**.
But in some Brāhmaṇa sentences precise identity of Aditi and the earth is stated. For example:—
"इयं वै पृथ्वी अदितिः सा इयं प्रतिष्ठा (*iyaṃ vai pṛthvī aditiḥ sā iyaṃ pratiṣṭhā*)" (Śa. Brā 2-2-1.19)
This earth is Aditi; she is the support of the world.
"इयं वै पृथ्वी अदितिः सा इयं देवानां पत्नि (*iyaṃ vai pṛthvī aditiḥ sā iyaṃ devānāṃ patni*)" (Śa. Brā. 5-3-1-4)
This earth is Aditi. She is the worshipful consort of the gods. Since there are Brāhmaṇa sentences of this kind, the precise meaning of the word Aditi must be stated only as suits each context.
It has been stated earlier that Yāska indicates that Aditi is the chief of all the deities dwelling in the atmosphere. But the same Yāska, grouping the Ādityas, the sons of Aditi, with the deities dwelling in heaven,

### Printed p. 31
while not grouping their mother Aditi alone with the deities dwelling in the atmosphere, does not appear altogether reasonable; but it cannot be said that Yāska made an effort at a precise classification.
**6. Aditi and Diti:—**
"O Mitra and Varuṇa, at dawn mount your golden chariot and look upon Aditi and Diti"
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 5.62-8)
In the mantra हिरण्यरूपं (*hiraṇyarūpaṃ*), which gives this meaning, Aditi is designated together with Diti. The two words Aditi and Diti are meaningful as showing a difference from each other; or else one fills up the deficiency of the other. In this way the mantra above indicates the relation and the difference of these two deities, who are mutually opposed in one sense and mutually complementary in another.
[The same words are used also in the Vājasaneyi Saṃhitā [10-16]. For this the commentator has stated the purport in the adhidaiva sense. For the word Aditi he has given the meaning "free from poverty" (adīna) and for the word Diti the meaning "poor" (dīna). That is, one who performs what is prescribed is Aditi; one of atheistic conduct is Diti. Such a difference has been shown.]
Sāyaṇa, in his commentary on the mantra Ṛ. Saṃ. 5.62.8, has explained Aditi as the indivisible earth, and Diti as the divisible earth, that is, the earth with individual creatures and the like. The German scholar Roth has explained the word Aditi as the eternal and the word Diti as the non-eternal.
(Journal of German Oriental Society. vol. VI p. 71)
Taking all the contexts together, it appears that Aditi may be called the deity reflecting the atmosphere and Diti the one reflecting the earth. It may also be said that these two principles together reflect the complete state which includes all the conditions of Nature.
For the word Diti used in the mantra Ṛ. Saṃ. 4-2-11, Sāyaṇa has given the meaning a generous giver, and for the word Aditi the meaning a miser who does not give. He has explained: दितिं— दातारं ; अदितिं— अदातुः पंचम्यर्थे द्वितीया ! (*ditiṃ— dātāraṃ ; aditiṃ— adātuḥ paṃcamyarthe dvitīyā !*).
There are also some contexts in which only Diti is used, without Aditi. For example—
"O Agni, O worshipful Savitṛ and Bhaga, grant us fame and offspring.

### Printed p. 32
Diti also grants us the things we desire"
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 7-15-12)
In the mantra त्वमग्ने (*tvamagne*), which gives this meaning, Sāyaṇa has given for the word Diti only the meaning "goddess". Here Diti is not joined with Aditi. In this context English or German scholars have supplied their own meaning for the word Diti according to their own imagination. For example, Roth has said: "Diti is a special deity reflecting either generosity or increase of wealth." Max Muller has stated: "In this context the word Aditi itself has been changed into Diti by a variant reading." In the mantras A. Ve. 15-6-7; 15-18-4; Vā. Saṃ. 18-22 it is precisely stated that Diti and Aditi are separate deities. In A. Ve. 7-7-1 it is indicated who are the sons of Diti and who are the sons of Aditi. These sons of Diti are later, in the Purāṇas and the like, regarded as Daityas and enemies of the gods.
**7. Aditi can be the deity-principle that reflects universal nature.**
There are also mantras which state that Aditi reflects Nature, which pervades the universe. Whatever things there are that belong to heaven and to the atmosphere, whatever beings there are, divine and human, whatever special principle there is that belongs to past, future, present and the rest, of all these Aditi is the origin, and she is the self of all.
“पदितिद्र्यौरदतिरंतरिक्षं........................आदितिर्जनित्वं (*paditidryauradatiraṃtarikṣaṃ........................āditirjanitvaṃ*)”
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-89-10)
The undivided Aditi is the giver of birth to the whole universe. She is the mother of the world, she is the father of the world. She is every thing that is born. She is the place of origin of all. She is the five kinds of people, those of the castes together with the Niṣādas. She is the gods, gandharvas and the rest. Aditi is all the gods. She is the shining heaven. She is the atmosphere, she is the whole universe. She is the governor of all that is born and that will be born.
Sāyaṇa, commenting on this sentence, says: "Here the word Aditi means either the earth or the mother of the gods", and explains her pervasion of the universe. आदितिरदीना अखंडनीया वा पृथ्वी देवमाता वा !............एवं सकलजगदात्मना अदितिस्तूयते (*āditiradīnā akhaṃḍanīyā vā pṛthvī devamātā vā !............evaṃ sakalajagadātmanā aditistūyate*). The author of the Nirukta, giving the explanation of this word, has explained it (अदितिरदीना देवमाता (*aditiradīnā devamātā*)) as the great-souled mother of the gods. (Ni. 4.22) In the mantra 1.89-10 quoted above, her greatness and the manifestation of her various forms are set out.

### Printed p. 33
"स्वयंभु ब्रह्म परमं तपो यत् । स एव पुत्रः स पिता स माता (*svayaṃbhu brahma paramaṃ tapo yat | sa eva putraḥ sa pitā sa mātā*)": the self-existent Supreme Brahman is the supremely pure austerity. He himself is the son, he himself is the mother, the father, and so on. Just as in this way all-selfhood and universal pervasion are described of Brahman (Tai. Brā. 3-12-3-1), in the same way Aditi is described in this mantra.
To know the greatness of the Aditi-principle, another very important context may be examined. The purport of that mantra is as follows—
"Who among the gods is the greatest? By remembering which god will this my distress be removed? When shall I see my father and my mother?" (1-24-1)
In the Aitareya Brāhmaṇa the same mantras 1-24-1 and 1-24-2 are quoted and it is stated that these mantras were recited by Śunaḥśepa. Some scholars differ in opinion as to whether this context has a close relation to Śunaḥśepa or not. That is not so important here. Whoever recited this mantra, it is a mantra to be recited by a man caught in calamity, with the desire to escape from the calamity.
**8. Aditi is the deity who forgives sin;—**
The scholar Benfey, in giving the meaning of the word Aditi in the mantras 1-24-1 and 1-24-2 quoted above, has stated for this word the meaning "freedom from sin" (Orient and Occident I-33). The purport that he has stated is, from one point of view, reasonable. That Aditi is the destroyer of sin is a well-known matter. That she is free from sin is also set out by epithets such as अनागस (*anāgasa*). That is why the prayer addressed to her is also of this kind.
"O Varuṇa, release all the bonds that are on my head and in the lower part. May we be sinless in respect of Aditi" (1-24-15)
In the same way, some sentences in which freedom from sin and purity of the self, obtained through the favour of Aditi, are set out are quoted below.
"May Aditi make us sinless" (1-166-22)
"O Aditi, O Mitra and Varuṇa, if we have committed any sin in respect of you, kindly forgive it and show us favour." (2-27-14)

### Printed p. 34
"O Agni, whatever fault we have committed through the thoughtlessness natural to men, make us sinless, so that the favour of Aditi may be obtained by us." (4-12-4)
"With the help of Savitṛ, who is of divine nature, may we not commit any sin in respect of Aditi." (5-82-6)
"Fully observing the resolved ordinance of Aditi, may we not commit any sin against Varuṇa. Varuṇa is of a nature to protect even the most sinful." (8-87-7)
"O Agni, whatever sin we have committed, forgive it with compassion towards us. May Aryaman **NOTE** and Aditi destroy that sin." (8-93-7)
"May Mitra hear my prayer. May Aditi and Savitṛ, of divine nature, declare us sinless before Varuṇa." (10-12-8)
"अनागास्त्वे आदितित्वे तुरास इमं यज्ञं दधतु शोषमाणाः । (*anāgāstve ādititve turāsa imaṃ yajñaṃ dadhatu śoṣamāṇāḥ |*)"
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 7-54-18)
In this mantra the two words अदितित्व (*adititva*) and अनागास्त्व (*anāgāstva*) are used together. The prayer is: "May the great gods, hearing our prayer, make our sacrificial rites free from sin and productive of prosperity." But one more point must be noticed here. Although the use of these two words is together, there is no rule that the two must have the same meaning. Showing a different explanation of the word Aditi, the Śatapatha Brāhmaṇa **NOTE**) says as follows.
स यद्यदेवासृजत तत्तदत्तुमध्रियत । सर्वं वा अत्तीति तददितेरदितित्वं । सर्वस्यैतस्यात्ता भवति । सर्वमस्यांतं भवति य एवमेतददितेरदितित्वं वेद (*sa yadyadevāsṛjata tattadattumadhriyata | sarvaṃ vā attīti tadaditeradititvaṃ | sarvasyaitasyāttā bhavati | sarvamasyāṃtaṃ bhavati ya evametadaditeradititvaṃ veda*)
(Śa. Brā. 10-6-55; Bṛ. U. 1-2-5)
Whatever things Brahman created, he began to devour them all again. Because of this act of devouring, the designation Aditi (death) came about.
